COPY package*.json ./

FROM node:22-alpine
# NODE_ENV (development | uat | production) also selects the Postgres CA file; set it per deployment.
# Mount the CA bundles at /app/backend/cert (dev.pem / uat.pem / prod.pem) or point POSTGRES_CERT_DIR elsewhere.
ENV NODE_ENV=production
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app/node_modules ./node_modules
COPY --from=build /app/dist ./dist
COPY package.json ./
RUN mkdir -p /app/logs && chown 1001:0 /app/logs
USER 1001
EXPOSE 3050
CMD ["node", "dist/index.js"]

/README.md
# Workspace Mgr

Middleware between the **Orion** dashboard and **Red Hat OpenShift Dev Spaces**. On demand of a user it
creates, lists, starts, stops and deletes DevWorkspaces, creates the user's project (namespace) when it does not
exist, manages per-user Git credentials, and records every action to Postgres for the Dev Spaces dashboard.

Node 20+, TypeScript (strict, ESM), Express 4, `@kubernetes/client-node`, `pg`, `zod`.
Project rules: [docs/PROJECT_RULES.md](docs/PROJECT_RULES.md) · Checklist: [docs/CHECKLIST.md](docs/CHECKLIST.md)

## Quick start

```bash
npm install
cp .env.example .env        # edit; SECRET_KEY, ADMIN_*, DEVREG_URL are required
npm run dev                 # tsx watch (loads env from your shell; use `node --env-file=.env` if you prefer)
npm run build && npm start  # production
```

| Script                                                | What it does                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `npm run typecheck` / `lint` / `format`               | tsc, eslint (0 warnings allowed), prettier |
| `npm test`                                            | unit + integration + e2e                   |
| `npm run test:unit` / `test:integration` / `test:e2e` | one layer                                  |
| `npm run test:coverage`                               | all layers with a coverage gate            |

## Postgres: the existing database

Metrics go into the **existing** Orion database. workspace-mgr creates nothing (no tables, schemas or migrations);
it only inserts rows into the table named by `POSTGRES_DATABASE_TABLENAME`.

Same variables as the original service:

```bash
export POSTGRES_DATABASE=orion_dev_metadata
export POSTGRES_HOSTNAME=NAORI01DPS_INSTANCE1.pgaas.dyn.nsroot.net
export POSTGRES_PASSWORD=...
export POSTGRES_PORT='1524'
export POSTGRES_USER=app_orion_pgsql_dev
export POSTGRES_DATABASE_TABLENAME='metrics."devspaces-metrics-dev"'
export NODE_ENV=development
```

- **TLS** is always on and the server certificate is verified, as before. `NODE_ENV` picks the CA file:
  `development` → `./certs/dev.pem` (hostname check relaxed), `uat` → `/app/backend/cert/uat.pem`,
  `production` → `/app/backend/cert/prod.pem`. Override with `POSTGRES_CERT_DIR` or `POSTGRES_CA_FILE`.
  The app refuses to start, naming the missing file, if the CA cannot be read.
- **Columns:** the service reads the table's column list once and writes only the columns that exist (it knows
  `timestamp, csiid, soeid, eventtype, resourcename, resourcetype, status, limitcpu, limitmemory, requestcpu,
requestmemory, duration, storage, useraction, errordetail, workspaceversion, editorname`). A table without
  `workspaceversion` / `editorname` still works; a warning names what is not stored.
- **Timestamps** are written as UTC wall-clock text, as the original did.
- A database problem never blocks a workspace action: it is logged and the action continues.

## Environment variables

Every variable the team exports is understood as-is (`.env.example` lists them with comments):

| Variable                                         | Meaning                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `PORT`                                           | HTTP port (default 3050)                                                                                                   |
| `NODE_ENV`                                       | `development` / `uat` / `production`; also picks the Postgres CA file                                                      |
| `SECRET_KEY`, `ADMIN_USERNAME`, `ADMIN_PASSWORD` | login and JWT signing; **required**, there is no fallback secret                                                           |
| `CLUSTER_API_URL`                                | Dev Spaces cluster API                                                                                                     |
| `devSpacesClusterToken001d`                      | its bearer token, found by name: the `001d` must end the cluster name (`namctigtd001d`)                                    |
| `DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL`, `devOpsToken004d`      | optional separate cluster for build/deploy projects and its token; unset = same cluster (a token for another id is unused) |
| `DEVREG_URL`                                     | devfile registry                                                                                                           |
| `EDITOR_VSCODE`, `EDITOR_INTELLIJ`               | full editor ids, e.g. `che-incubator/che-code/latest`                                                                      |
| `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0`                 | do not verify certificates (clusters and every HTTPS peer); a warning is logged at startup                                 |
| `POSTGRES_*`                                     | see "Postgres: the existing database"                                                                                      |

Without a token the service uses `KUBECONFIG_PATH` (or the first command-line argument), then the default kubeconfig /
pod service account. `CLUSTER_TOKEN` and `DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN` set a token explicitly. The Dev Spaces dashboard address
for the editor link is `DEV_SPACES_SERVICE_FQDN`, or `https://devspaces.apps.<cluster domain>` worked out from `CLUSTER_API_URL`.
At startup the service logs what it understood (which variable supplied each token, never the token itself).

## Logs

The terminal shows one readable entry per event: `time | LEVEL | event | details`, the whole line coloured.

| Colour | Meaning                                                                                                 |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| green  | something succeeded: a call that returned 2xx/3xx, a workspace created/started/stopped/deleted, startup |
| orange | warnings: a call the caller got wrong (4xx), TLS checking switched off, a table with missing columns    |
| red    | errors: a call that failed on our side (5xx) and its cause, a failed e-mail, a database write failure   |
| white  | everything else (information, debug)                                                                    |

```text
2026-10-24 00:55:44.025 | OK    | request                        | GET    /api/v1/healthz -> 200 (6 ms)  requestId=258d…
2026-10-24 00:55:44.366 | WARN  | request                        | POST   /api/v1/login -> 401 (2 ms)  requestId=dc21…
2026-10-24 00:55:44.479 | ERROR | request.failed
    requestId : d348d911-a444-4803-bc94-3b3affad830b
    code      : UPSTREAM_ERROR
    error     : Devfile registry catalog: No response received from server
```

Entries with a lot of detail (startup summary, errors) use indented `key : value` lines followed by a blank line, so
entries never run together. Secrets (tokens, passwords) are never printed. `LOG_FORMAT=json` gives one JSON object
per line for a log collector (a success carries `"outcome":"success"`), `NO_COLOR=1` or `LOG_COLOR=false` removes the
colours, `LOG_LEVEL=debug|info|warn|error|silent` filters.

## API (version 1)

All routes live under **`/api/v1`**. Send `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` except for `login`.
Errors are always `{ "error": string, "message": string, "code": string }`.

| Method | Path                                    | Purpose                                                        |
| ------ | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| POST   | `/api/v1/login`                         | `{username,password}` → `{token}`                              |
| GET    | `/api/v1/catalog`                       | Stacks from the devfile registry                               |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/projects`               | The user's projects (namespaces)                               |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces`             | List workspaces                                                |
| POST   | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces`             | Create a workspace (creates the project if needed)             |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id`         | Workspace details                                              |
| DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id`         | Delete                                                         |
| POST   | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id/start`   | Start                                                          |
| POST   | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id/stop`    | Stop                                                           |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats`                   | List GitHub PAT secrets                                        |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats/:tokenName?csiid=` | View (token is masked)                                         |
| POST   | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats/:tokenName`        | `{csiid,token}` create (409 if it exists)                      |
| PUT    | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats/:tokenName`        | `{csiid,token}` replace                                        |
| DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats/:tokenName?csiid=` | Delete (idempotent)                                            |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/gitconfig?csiid=`       | View `~/.gitconfig`                                            |
| PUT    | `/api/v1/:soeid/gitconfig`              | `{csiid,content}` create or replace                            |
| DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/gitconfig?csiid=`       | Delete (idempotent)                                            |
| GET    | `/api/v1/:soeid/deployment-projects`    | List build/deploy projects                                     |
| POST   | `/api/v1/:soeid/deployment-projects`    | `{csiid}` → 201 created, or 200 if it already exists           |
| DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/deployment-projects`    | Delete all of the user's deployment projects                   |
| GET    | `/api/v1/healthz`, `/api/v1/readyz`                   | Liveness (no dependencies) / readiness (Kubernetes + Postgres) |

Create-workspace body:

```json
{
  "devworkspaceValue": "java-maven",
  "csiid": "177398",
  "editor": "vscode",
  "stackVersion": "2.0.0",
  "repositoryUrl": "https://github.com/acme/app.git",
  "repositoryBranch": "main"
}
```

`editor` is `vscode` (default), `intellij` or `goland`; the last three fields are optional.

**Versioning.** Every endpoint is served only under `/api/v1`, including health/readiness probes. Unversioned URLs return 404. See [API and code map](docs/API.md) for routers, controllers, administrative APIs, runtime configuration and log search.

## Architecture

```
app → routers/index → feature routers → controllers  →  services (rules)  →  ports ← infra (Kubernetes, Postgres, registry, email)
                                         ↓
                                   factories (pure) , utils , constants
```

`src/container.ts` is the only place adapters are wired to services; tests wire in fakes
(`tests/helpers/InMemoryKube.ts` behaves like a cluster, `pg-mem` like Postgres).

## Tests

| Layer       | Where               | What is real                                                                                       |
| ----------- | ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Unit        | `tests/unit`        | One module; everything else faked                                                                  |
| Integration | `tests/integration` | Express routes + services + SQL (`pg-mem`, with a test-only table fixture) + HTTP devfile registry |
| End to end  | `tests/e2e`         | A listening server driven only over HTTP through whole user journeys                               |

The cluster is always in memory: nothing here talks to a real OpenShift. Run a smoke test against your dev
cluster before first release (see "Not verified").

## Hand-over to another computer (`recreate-app.sh`, `all-app-code.txt`)

The app moves between computers as **one text file**, `all-app-code.txt`, and one script, `recreate-app.sh`.
Build the text file with `tools/build-all-code.sh`; on the destination computer, run:

```bash
bash recreate-app.sh -f             # inside the app folder: writes the files of ./all-app-code.txt, overwriting
bash recreate-app.sh -f --prune     # also delete files that were removed from the app
bash recreate-app.sh -n             # dry run: show what would happen
bash recreate-app.sh p.txt ./out    # another input file / another folder (existing files are kept without -f)
```

- **First hand-over:** the entire application. **Every later one:** only the new and changed files, plus a list of
  files that were removed (`/_REMOVED.txt`). It ends with what to run next (`npm install`, `npm run build`, ...) when
  something relevant changed.
- **New empty folder:** put `recreate-app.sh` and `all-app-code.txt` in it and run `bash recreate-app.sh -f`, then
  `npm ci && npm test && npm run build && npm start`.
- **Format:** `/path/to/file`, the file's content, a blank line, next file. Files that cannot be shown as exact plain
  text (CRLF line endings, no final newline, odd names) are stored as base64 after `@@BINARY-SECTION`; the plain files
  are LF, so an editor, e-mail or git changing line endings cannot damage anything.
- **Safe:** the text file ends with a SHA-256 for every file; they are all checked in a temp folder before your files are
  touched. Existing files that differ are kept unless you pass `-f`. Paths that could escape the folder are rejected.
  `recreate-app.sh` never replaces itself while running; a newer version is saved as `recreate-app.sh.new`.
- **Never in a hand-over:** `node_modules`, `dist`, `coverage`, `.git`, `tools`, `.env*` (secrets), private keys.
- **For the maintainer:** `bash tools/build-all-code.sh` builds the next hand-over (entire app the first time, then
  only changes; `--full`, `--list`, `--redo` and `--keep-state` exist). What the other computer has is remembered in
  `.handover-state` (not committed). Before saving, the file is recreated in a temp folder and compared byte for byte.

## Assumptions (my defaults; please confirm)

You did not show the create-workspace payload, `index.ts`, `updateDevfile.ts` or the first three screenshots, so
these were decided without them:

- **Payload** is the shape above. `devworkspaceValue`+`csiid` come from the legacy code; the rest are additions.
- **Editor profiles:** JetBrains server ports `2222` (IntelliJ) and `3400` (GoLand), home volume `10Gi`. Read from
  a partly visible screenshot; verify in `src/factories/devfile-updater.ts`.
- **Workspaces are inlined** in the DevWorkspace (`spec.template` + `contributions`) rather than creating a separate
  DevWorkspaceTemplate as the oldest code did. Delete still cleans up legacy templates.
- **Limits:** 3 running / 5 total workspaces per user (`MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES`, `MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES`).
- **Missing config files** (`user-namespace.yaml`, `user-role-default.yaml`) are rebuilt in code
  (`src/constants/kubernetes-configs.ts`, `NamespaceService`). The user role there is my guess.
- **Auth:** only the admin login exists, as before; its token may act for any `:soeid` (what Orion needs today).
  Tokens with `role: user` are limited to their own `:soeid`.
- **Status codes** follow REST: login failure is `401` (was `400`), PAT create is `201`.
- **Git username** is `<soeid><GITHUB_USER_SUFFIX>` (legacy used `<soeid>_citi`).

## Not verified

- No run against a real OpenShift cluster, real Postgres, or the real devfile registry/mail/GraphQL services.
- `docker build` was not run. The Helm chart (`helm/`) has no Deployment template and its folder is named
  `template`, not `templates`; it was left unchanged.

/docs/API.md
# Workspace Manager API and code map

Every endpoint starts with `/api/v1`. Unversioned URLs return 404. There is no legacy
router, compatibility switch, or archived implementation to maintain.

## Finding the code

```text
src/index.ts                   start server, load settings, graceful shutdown
src/app.ts                     Express setup, request logging, error handling
src/routers/index.ts           mount all feature routers and authentication
src/routers/*Router.ts         HTTP methods and paths
src/controllers/*Controller.ts parse requests and produce responses
src/services/                  provisioning and workspace business rules
src/http/helpers.ts             shared parameters and administrator authorization
src/config/                    environment validation and daily log storage
src/infra/                     Kubernetes, registry, email and database adapters
src/types.ts                   shared resource and API types
src/utils/                     shared logging, errors, names and resources
tests/unit/                    isolated behavior
tests/integration/             API, service and adapter wiring
tests/e2e/                     complete HTTP journeys
```

Source files are TypeScript; the build emits ESM `.js` files in `dist/`.
`npm start` runs `dist/index.js`, which calls the application factory in `dist/app.js`.
To debug a route, follow its router to the matching controller and service.

## Endpoints

Paths below include the version prefix. Use `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` for all
routes except login and probes. The existing login issues an administrator token;
regular user tokens can access only their own `:soeid` resources.

| Method                 | Path                                  | Purpose                                               |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/healthz`                     | Liveness; does not contact dependencies               |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/readyz`                      | Kubernetes, deployment cluster and database readiness |
| POST                   | `/api/v1/login`                       | Existing username/password login                      |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/catalog`                     | Stack catalog                                         |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/:soeid/stacks`               | Stack catalog for an authorized user                  |
| GET, POST              | `/api/v1/:soeid/projects`             | List or provision workspace projects                  |
| GET, POST              | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces`           | List or create workspaces                             |
| GET, DELETE            | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id`       | Read or terminate a workspace                         |
| POST                   | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id/start` | Start a workspace                                     |
| POST                   | `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces/:id/stop`  | Stop a workspace                                      |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats`                 | List PATs                                             |
| GET, POST, PUT, DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/pats/:tokenName`      | View, create, update or delete a PAT                  |
| GET, POST, PUT, DELETE | `/api/v1/:soeid/gitconfig`            | View, upsert or delete Git configuration              |
| GET, POST, DELETE      | `/api/v1/:soeid/deployment-projects`  | List, provision or delete deployment projects         |
| PUT                    | `/api/v1/support/resources`           | Administrator resource reconciliation                 |
| GET, PATCH             | `/api/v1/configuration`               | Administrator runtime settings                        |
| GET                    | `/api/v1/logs`                        | Administrator log search and formatted output         |

Project/workspace/deployment-project lists accept an optional `?csiid=177398`
filter. Identifiers use the application's existing names: **SOEID** and **CSIID**.
Stacks come from the configured registry and are shared across projects.

`POST /api/v1/:soeid/projects` and `POST /api/v1/:soeid/deployment-projects` accept
`{ "csiid": "177398" }`. They reuse the existing provisioning services, including
roles, bindings, quotas, limit ranges, workspace network policies and proxy configuration.
Creation returns 201; an existing project returns 200. Deployment projects use the
configured deployment cluster. Deleting deployment projects retains the existing
behavior: delete all matching deployment projects for that user.

PAT writes accept `{ "csiid": "177398", "token": "..." }`. PAT reads and deletes use
`?csiid=177398`. Git configuration writes accept `{ "csiid": "177398", "content": "..." }`;
reads and deletes use the same query parameter. POST and PUT Git configuration both upsert.

## Repairing or updating Kubernetes resources

Restore existing defaults on a user's workspace project:

```http
PUT /api/v1/support/resources
Authorization: Bearer <admin-jwt>
Content-Type: application/json

{ "soeid": "pp58386", "csiid": "177398", "target": "workspace" }
```

Use `"target": "deployment"` for the deployment cluster/project. The project must
exist and its owner annotation must match the requested SOEID. Omitting `resources`
reapplies the existing provisioning defaults, including network/proxy resources for
workspace projects. This repairs missing resources without recreating a project.

To create or replace selected resources, supply their manifests:

```json
{
  "soeid": "pp58386",
  "csiid": "177398",
  "target": "workspace",
  "resources": [
    {
      "kind": "ResourceQuota",
      "metadata": { "name": "custom-compute-quota" },
      "spec": { "hard": { "requests.cpu": "4", "requests.memory": "8Gi" } }
    }
  ]
}
```

Allowed kinds: `Role`, `RoleBinding`, `ResourceQuota`, `LimitRange`, `ClusterRole`,
`ClusterRoleBinding`. `metadata` accepts only `name`: the server sets namespace and
API version. Cluster roles/bindings are cluster-scoped and apply to the selected
workspace or deployment cluster. Kubernetes has no cluster-scoped LimitRange or
ResourceQuota; those apply to the selected user's namespace on that cluster.
Kubernetes validates manifest rules, subjects, role references and specs.

Operations run sequentially. If one fails, earlier successful updates remain;
correct the error and retry. Existing Kubernetes permissions still apply. The
service account needs explicit cluster-RBAC write privileges for cluster-scoped
operations; this change does not automatically expand deployment privileges.

## Runtime settings

`GET /api/v1/configuration` returns settings with passwords and tokens redacted.
`PATCH /api/v1/configuration` accepts any nonempty subset of:

```json
{
  "DEVREG_URL": "https://registry.example.com",
  "NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL": "https://mail.example.com",
  "NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME": "workspace-mailer",
  "NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD": "replacement-password",
  "MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES": 3,
  "MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES": 5,
  "CLUSTER_API_URL": "https://api.cluster.example.com:6443",
  "CLUSTER_TOKEN": "replacement-token",
  "DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL": "https://api.deploy.example.com:6443",
  "DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN": "replacement-deployment-token"
}
```

Changes take effect for subsequent requests in this process. Production adapters
are rebuilt so updated URLs and credentials are used; the database pool remains
open. In-flight operations can finish using their original services. Updates are
validated before replacement. Running limits cannot exceed total limits; changing
a cluster URL requires its token in the same request.

**Persistence is process-lifetime:** this API does not rewrite deployment environment
variables, Kubernetes Secrets or `.env` files. Set the same values in deployment
configuration to retain them after restart and across replicas. Responses explicitly
report `"persistence": "process-lifetime"`. No arbitrary environment-variable writes
are supported. Stored secret values are never returned by this API.

## Troubleshooting with daily logs

Production writes one redacted JSON entry per line to `logs/app-YYYY-MM-DD.log`.
Each entry includes `timestamp` (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` in the process's local timezone),
`file`, and `function`. The ISO `time` field remains available for precise filtering.
Readable console and log-search output uses
`[timestamp] [File: filename] [Function: functionName]`. Continuation lines carry the
same prefix. Source details come from the log call, or the original error stack when
an Error object is provided; unnamed callbacks appear as `anonymous`.
Dates are UTC and files switch automatically at midnight. Console formatting is
independent. `LOG_DIRECTORY` selects the directory. Mount a writable volume there
when using a read-only filesystem, and persistent storage if logs must survive pod
replacement. Log files are not committed. Retention is managed by the deployment.

```http
GET /api/v1/logs?days=2&level=error&search=namespace&limit=100&format=text
Authorization: Bearer <admin-jwt>
```

Supported query parameters:

| Parameter   | Meaning                                           |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| `days`      | Rolling last 1–31 days; default 1                 |
| `limit`     | Maximum 1–1000 results; default 200               |
| `level`     | `debug`, `info`, `warn`, or `error`               |
| `search`    | Case-insensitive text match, up to 200 characters |
| `requestId` | Exact request correlation ID                      |
| `soeid`     | Exact user match                                  |
| `format`    | `json` (default) or readable `text`               |

Results are newest first and include the queried range and a `truncated` indicator.
Search scans at most 100,000 lines per request. Missing files and malformed lines are
ignored. Search uses literal text filtering. The API searches this
instance's files; aggregate logs externally when running multiple replicas.

## Offline transfer

Copy `recreate-app.sh` and `all-app-code.txt` into the destination application folder:

```bash
bash recreate-app.sh -n -f --prune
bash recreate-app.sh -f --prune
```

The first command previews the update. The second verifies checksums, writes the
snapshot and deletes files explicitly listed as removed. The snapshot contains the
complete application source, tests, lockfile, configuration templates and documentation.
It can also be restored into an empty folder.

Node.js, installed dependencies and secrets are not included. Install dependencies
from your configured internal npm repository with `npm ci`, then run `npm run build`.
Supply environment variables or mounted secrets separately.

To prepare another full transfer on the development machine, run
`bash tools/build-all-code.sh --full`. Its verification restores every bundled file
to a temporary directory and compares it byte for byte with the source.

## Verification

Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test -- --coverage`, and `npm run build`.
Tests retain the existing service and user journeys, replace deprecated-path checks
with 404 assertions, and cover provisioning, support updates on both clusters,
administrator restrictions, runtime settings, and daily log retrieval.

/docs/PROJECT_RULES.md
# Workspace Mgr — Project Rules

Workspace Mgr is the backend/middleware between the Orion dashboard and OpenShift
Dev Spaces. It creates, lists, starts, stops and deletes DevWorkspaces for a user,
creates the user's project (namespace) when it does not exist, and records every
action to Postgres for the Dev Spaces dashboard.

These rules are binding for every change. If a rule must be broken, say why in the PR.

## 1. Architecture

1. **Layers, strictly top-down:** `routers` → `controllers` → `services` → `infra`/`factories` → `types`/`utils`.
   A lower layer never imports from a higher one.
2. **`controllers`** only parses/validates input, calls one service method, and maps the result or
   error to a response. No Kubernetes, SQL or business rules in routes.
3. **`services`** own business rules (limits, ordering of steps, naming, metrics events).
   They depend on **ports** (interfaces), never on concrete clients.
4. **`infra`** holds the concrete adapters (Kubernetes, Postgres, devfile registry, email).
   Every adapter implements a port declared in `src/infra/ports.ts`.
5. **`factories`** are pure functions (devfile → DevWorkspace, log records). No I/O.
6. **One composition root** (`src/container.ts`) wires adapters into services. Nothing else
   calls `new` on an adapter. Tests build the container with fakes.
7. No module-level side effects (no connecting to a DB, reading files, or reading `process.env`
   at import time). Startup config is read in `src/config/env.ts` and passed in; administrator runtime updates rebuild services.

## 2. Language and style

1. TypeScript `strict: true`, ESM (`"module": "NodeNext"`). Local imports end in `.js`.
2. No `any` except at a third-party boundary, and then it is narrowed immediately.
   **No `// @ts-ignore`.** Use `// @ts-expect-error` with a reason if truly unavoidable.
3. Prefer `async/await`. No `.then().catch()` chains.
4. Functions are small and single-purpose. A service method that needs a comment block to
   explain its steps should be split.
5. Names: `camelCase` values, `PascalCase` types/classes, feature-matched `*Router.ts` / `*Controller.ts` filenames for HTTP modules; `kebab-case.ts` for other modules
   that export functions, `PascalCase.ts` for files whose main export is a class.
6. Comments explain _why_, not _what_. No commented-out code, no TODO without an issue id.
7. `eslint` and `prettier` must pass with zero warnings.

## 3. Errors

1. Throw typed errors from `src/utils/errors.ts` (`AppError` subclasses). Each carries an HTTP
   status and a stable `code`.
2. Never swallow an error silently. Either handle it, record it, or rethrow it.
   The only intentional swallow is the metrics recorder (rule 6.2), and it logs.
3. Kubernetes/axios errors are translated to `AppError` at the adapter boundary
   (`src/utils/k8s-errors.ts`). Callers never inspect `err.body.code`.
4. Error responses always have the shape `{ "error": string, "message": string, "code": string }`.
   Never return a stack trace or upstream response body to the client.

## 4. Security

1. `SECRET_KEY` is **required**; the process refuses to start without it. No fallback secret.
2. Tokens: `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` only. Verify with the explicit algorithm `HS256`.
3. Admin credential comparison is constant-time (`crypto.timingSafeEqual`).
4. Every `:soeid` and `:id` path parameter is validated against an allow-list pattern before it
   is used in a namespace name, label selector or Kubernetes path.
5. A caller may only act on their own `:soeid` unless the token has the `admin` role.
6. Never log tokens, passwords, PAT values, or full request bodies. PAT values are masked
   (`ghp_ab…xyz`) everywhere except the Secret itself.
7. Secrets for Kubernetes and Postgres come from the environment / mounted files, never from
   source or from git.
8. All SQL is parameterised. No string-built queries.
9. Kubernetes access uses the pod's service account with the minimum RBAC in
   `kustomize.server/base/sa.yaml`; do not widen it without review.

## 5. Kubernetes behaviour

1. Operations are idempotent: "create or replace" for quotas, roles, bindings, ConfigMaps.
2. Workspace namespaces are found by **label + annotation**, not by listing the whole cluster.
   Deployment namespaces are created through OpenShift's `ProjectRequest`, which may not apply our labels, so
   they are found by their deterministic name (`<soeid>-devspaces-deploy-<csiid>`).
3. A workspace id is `metadata.name`; it is unique inside a user's namespaces.
4. Poll loops have a hard timeout and an interval, are injectable (clock/sleep) for tests, and
   fail with a typed error. They never silently "fix" state by restarting workspaces.
5. Deleting something that is already gone is a success.

## 6. Metrics (Postgres)

1. Every user-visible action (create/delete/start/stop of workspace; create/delete of project;
   PAT/gitconfig changes) records one row to `metrics."devspaces-metrics"`, success **or** failure.
2. Recording is best-effort: a database failure is logged and must never fail or delay the
   user's action beyond a short timeout.
3. The row shape is defined once (`MetricsRecord`). The metrics table **already exists** and is owned by the
   database team: the service never issues DDL (no `CREATE`, `ALTER`, `DROP`) and ships no migrations. It only
   `INSERT`s, plus read-only `SELECT`s of the table's column list so it can write into an older table that lacks
   some of our columns. A test asserts this. The table SQL under `tests/helpers/` is a test fixture only.
4. Postgres is configured only through the existing variables (`POSTGRES_DATABASE`, `_HOSTNAME`, `_PASSWORD`,
   `_PORT`, `_USER`, `_DATABASE_TABLENAME`) and `NODE_ENV`, which picks the CA file (dev/uat/prod) as before.
5. Timestamps are written as UTC wall-clock text (`2026-01-02 03:04:05.678`), as the original service did. The
   CST format is for logs and emails only.

## 7. Testing

1. Three layers, all required for a feature:
   - **Unit** (`tests/unit`): one module, all collaborators faked, no network, no timers
     (use fake clock). Fast (< 5 s total).
   - **Integration** (`tests/integration`): real HTTP routes + real services + real SQL
     (`pg-mem`) + in-memory Kubernetes + a local fake devfile registry. Verifies wiring.
   - **End to end** (`tests/e2e`): a real listening server, driven only over HTTP
     (`fetch`) through whole user journeys (login → create → start → stop → delete), then
     asserting the Kubernetes state and metrics rows.
2. A bug fix starts with a failing test.
3. Tests do not depend on order, wall-clock time, or real cluster/DB access.
4. Coverage gate: ≥ 85 % lines and ≥ 80 % branches on `src/` (excluding `src/index.ts`).
5. No `.only`, no `.skip` committed.

## 8. API contract

1. **URI versioning, industry standard:** every API route is served under `/api/v1`
   (`/api/v1/login`, `/api/v1/catalog`, `/api/v1/:soeid/workspaces[/:id[/start|/stop]]`, ...).
   Path structure after the prefix is identical to the pre-versioning layout.
   Operational probes also use the version prefix.
2. Within a major version changes are additive only (new routes, new optional fields).
   A breaking change ships as `/api/v2` alongside `v1`; `v1` is then deprecated, never edited in place.
3. Unversioned routes return 404. There is no legacy router or compatibility switch.
4. Request bodies are validated with `zod`; unknown properties are rejected.
5. `409` for limit/conflict, `404` unknown resource, `400` invalid input, `401/403` auth,
   `502` upstream (cluster/registry) failure, `500` everything else.

## 9. Operations

1. `/api/v1/healthz` (liveness) never touches dependencies. `/api/v1/readyz` checks Kubernetes API and
   Postgres reachability.
2. Logs are one entry per event with `requestId`, `soeid`, `event`. Terminal format (`LOG_FORMAT=pretty`, the default) is readable and coloured: errors red, warnings orange, successes green, everything else white; `LOG_FORMAT=json` gives structured JSON lines. Use `logger.success` for something that worked, `warn` for a problem the caller or the setup caused, `error` for a failure on our side.
3. Graceful shutdown on `SIGTERM`: stop accepting, drain, close the DB pool.
4. Startup config uses environment variables documented in `.env.example`. Administrator runtime settings are process-local; see `API.md`.
5. Container runs as non-root, read-only root filesystem compatible.

## 10. Git and review

1. Small, focused commits; imperative subject line.
2. CI order: `typecheck` → `lint` → `test:unit` → `test:integration` → `test:e2e` → `build`.
3. A PR is mergeable only when the [CHECKLIST](./CHECKLIST.md) items for its scope are ticked.

);

/recreate-app.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# recreate-app.sh
#
# Writes the files of all-app-code.txt into the app folder. The first hand-over holds the ENTIRE application;
# after that the file holds only what changed since the last hand-over (new files, changed files, a removal list).
# It is made by build-all-code.sh.
#
# all-app-code.txt format:
#     /relative/path/to/file.ext      <- path line (starts with "/")
#     ...file content...
#                                     <- one blank separator line
#     /_REMOVED.txt                   <- (only when needed) files that no longer belong to the app, one per line
#     @@BINARY-SECTION                <- (only when needed) files that cannot be shown as plain text (CRLF files,
#     @@FILE /docs/img/x.png             no final newline, odd names, pictures ...) as base64 text:
#         ...base64 lines...             then  @@END
#     @@CHECKSUMS                     <- SHA-256 of every file in the hand-over; checked BEFORE anything is written
#
# Usage (run it inside the app folder):
#     bash recreate-app.sh -f                      # reads ./all-app-code.txt, overwrites what is there   <- normal use
#     bash recreate-app.sh -f p.txt                # another input file name
#     bash recreate-app.sh -f --prune              # also delete the files listed under /_REMOVED.txt
#     bash recreate-app.sh p.txt ./out             # write into another folder (existing files are kept without -f)
#     bash recreate-app.sh -n                      # dry run: show what would happen, write nothing
#     bash recreate-app.sh -l                      # list the files in the hand-over
#     bash recreate-app.sh -f --no-verify          # skip the checksum check (only for a text file you edited by hand)
#
# Without -f existing files that differ are NOT overwritten (they are reported and skipped); identical ones are left alone.
# Safe against line-ending changes: if an editor, e-mail or git converts the text file to CRLF, nothing is damaged.
# At the end it tells you what to run next (npm install, npm run build ...) - only when it is needed.

set -euo pipefail
export LC_ALL=C

SELF="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)/$(basename "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")"

hash_tool() {
  if command -v sha256sum >/dev/null 2>&1; then echo sha256sum
  elif command -v shasum >/dev/null 2>&1; then echo "shasum -a 256"
  fi
}

usage() { sed -n '2,30p' "$SELF" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'; }

# Everything lives in main() and the script ends with "exit" on the same line that calls it, so bash never reads
# the file again after main starts: a hand-over may safely contain a newer version of this very script.
main() {
  local FORCE=0 PRUNE=0 DRY=0 LIST=0 NOVERIFY=0 a
  local POS=()
  for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
      -f|--force) FORCE=1 ;;
      --prune) PRUNE=1 ;;
      --no-verify) NOVERIFY=1 ;;
      -n|--dry-run) DRY=1 ;;
      -l|--list) LIST=1 ;;
      -h|--help) usage; return 0 ;;
      -*) echo "ERROR: unknown option: $a" >&2; return 1 ;;
      *) POS+=("$a") ;;
    esac
  done

  local INPUT="${POS[0]:-all-app-code.txt}"
  local TARGET="${POS[1]:-.}"
  if [ ! -f "$INPUT" ]; then
    echo "ERROR: input file not found: $INPUT" >&2
    return 1
  fi
  # Windows-style target (C:\dir) under Git Bash
  if command -v cygpath >/dev/null 2>&1 && printf '%s' "$TARGET" | grep -Eq '^[A-Za-z]:[\\/]'; then
    TARGET="$(cygpath -u "$TARGET")"
  fi

  STAGE="$(mktemp -d)"            # global on purpose: the EXIT trap runs after main() has returned
  trap 'rm -rf "$STAGE"' EXIT
  mkdir -p "$STAGE/files"
  : > "$STAGE/list"; : > "$STAGE/removed"; : > "$STAGE/binlist"; : > "$STAGE/sums"

  # ---- 1. plain-text files and the removal list -> staging (nothing touches the target yet) ---------------
  # BINMODE=3: gawk on Windows (Git Bash) must not translate line endings; every CR is removed explicitly below.
  awk -v BINMODE=3 -v stage="$STAGE/files" -v listf="$STAGE/list" -v remf="$STAGE/removed" '
  function shq(s) { gsub(/\047/, "\047\\\047\047", s); return "\047" s "\047" }
  function safe_path(p,   n, parts, i) {
    if (p !~ /^\//) return 0
    if (p ~ /\/\// || p ~ /\\/ || p ~ /\/$/) return 0
    n = split(p, parts, "/")
    for (i = 2; i <= n; i++) if (parts[i] == ".." || parts[i] == ".") return 0
    return 1
  }
  # A path line: starts with a single "/", no spaces, a normal file name
  # (has an extension, or is a well-known extensionless name).
  function is_path(l,    base, n, parts) {
    if (l !~ /^\/[A-Za-z0-9_.@~+\-]/) return 0
    if (l ~ /^\/\//) return 0
    if (l ~ /^\/\*/) return 0
    if (l ~ /[ \t]/) return 0
    if (l !~ /^\/[A-Za-z0-9_.@~+\/\-]+$/) return 0
    if (!safe_path(l)) return 0
    n = split(l, parts, "/")
    base = parts[n]
    if (base == "") return 0
    if (base ~ /\./) return 1
    if (base == "Dockerfile" || base == "Makefile" || base == "LICENSE" || base == "Procfile") return 1
    return 0
  }
  function open_file(path,    full, dir) {
    if (cur != "") close(cur)
    removing = 0
    if (path == "/_REMOVED.txt") { removing = 1; cur = ""; return }     # a list, not a file
    full = stage path
    dir = full
    sub(/\/[^\/]*$/, "", dir)
    system("mkdir -p " shq(dir))
    cur = full
    printf "" > cur                      # create / truncate
    print path >> listf
  }
  BEGIN { stop = 0; held = 0; started = 0; cur = ""; removing = 0 }
  {
    line = $0
    sub(/\r$/, "", line)

    if (line == "@@BINARY-SECTION" || line == "@@CHECKSUMS") stop = 1   # base64 files and checksums follow
    if (stop) next

    if (line == "") {
      if (held && cur != "") printf "\n" >> cur
      held = 1
      next
    }
    if ((!started || held) && is_path(line)) {
      started = 1
      held = 0                           # this blank line was the separator
      open_file(line)
      next
    }
    if (!started) next                   # ignore anything before the first path (the comment header)
    if (removing) {                      # inside /_REMOVED.txt
      held = 0
      if (is_path(line)) print line >> remf
      next
    }
    if (held) {
      if (cur != "") printf "\n" >> cur
      held = 0
    }
    if (cur != "") print line >> cur
  }
  END { if (cur != "") close(cur) }
  ' "$INPUT"

  # ---- 2. base64 files -> staging ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  if grep -q '^@@BINARY-SECTION' "$INPUT"; then
    awk -v BINMODE=3 -v stage="$STAGE/files" -v listf="$STAGE/list" -v binf="$STAGE/binlist" '
    function shq(s) { gsub(/\047/, "\047\\\047\047", s); return "\047" s "\047" }
    function safe_path(p,   n, parts, i) {
      if (p !~ /^\//) return 0
      if (p ~ /\/\// || p ~ /\\/ || p ~ /\/$/) return 0
      n = split(p, parts, "/")
      for (i = 2; i <= n; i++) if (parts[i] == ".." || parts[i] == ".") return 0
      return 1
    }
    { sub(/\r$/, "") }
    /^@@BINARY-SECTION/ { on = 1; next }
    /^@@CHECKSUMS/ { if (out != "") close(out); out = ""; on = 0; next }
    !on { next }
    /^@@END/ { if (out != "") close(out); out = ""; next }
    /^@@FILE / {
      if (out != "") close(out)
      p = substr($0, 8)
      if (!safe_path(p)) { print "ERROR: unsafe path in the hand-over: " p > "/dev/stderr"; bad = 1; exit 1 }
      full = stage p; dir = full; sub(/\/[^\/]*$/, "", dir)
      system("mkdir -p " shq(dir))
      out = full ".b64"; printf "" > out
      print p >> listf; print full >> binf
      next
    }
    { if (out != "") print $0 >> out }
    END { if (out != "") close(out); if (bad) exit 1 }
    ' "$INPUT"
    local f
    while IFS= read -r f; do
      if base64 -d "$f.b64" > "$f" 2>/dev/null; then rm -f "$f.b64"
      else echo "ERROR: could not decode base64 data for ${f#"$STAGE/files"}; nothing was written" >&2; return 1; fi
    done < "$STAGE/binlist"
  fi

  # ---- 3. check every file against its SHA-256 BEFORE writing anything -------------------------------------
  awk -v BINMODE=3 '
    { sub(/\r$/, "") }
    /^@@CHECKSUMS/ { on = 1; next }
    on && NF >= 2 { print $1 "\t" substr($0, length($1) + 3) }
  ' "$INPUT" > "$STAGE/sums"

  local tool; tool="$(hash_tool)"
  if [ -s "$STAGE/sums" ] && [ -n "$tool" ]; then
    local sum path got badn=0 bad1=""
    while IFS=$'\t' read -r sum path; do
      [ -n "$path" ] || continue
      if [ ! -f "$STAGE/files$path" ]; then got="(missing)"; else got="$($tool "$STAGE/files$path" | cut -d' ' -f1)"; fi
      if [ "$got" != "$sum" ]; then badn=$((badn + 1)); [ -n "$bad1" ] || bad1="$path"; fi
    done < "$STAGE/sums"
    # a file without a checksum would be unverified (hand-added, or a damaged text file)
    cut -f2 "$STAGE/sums" | sort -u > "$STAGE/sums.paths"
    sort -u "$STAGE/list" | comm -23 - "$STAGE/sums.paths" > "$STAGE/nosum"
    if [ -s "$STAGE/nosum" ]; then
      badn=$((badn + $(grep -c . "$STAGE/nosum")))
      [ -n "$bad1" ] || bad1="$(head -n1 "$STAGE/nosum") (no checksum)"
    fi
    if [ "$badn" -gt 0 ]; then
      if [ "$NOVERIFY" = 1 ]; then
        echo "warning: $badn file(s) did not match their checksum (first: $bad1); continuing because of --no-verify" >&2
      else
        echo "ERROR: checksum mismatch for $bad1$([ "$badn" -gt 1 ] && echo " and $((badn - 1)) more"): the text file was edited or damaged in transit. Nothing was written." >&2
        echo "       (a deliberate hand edit can be applied with --no-verify)" >&2
        return 1
      fi
    fi
  elif [ "$NOVERIFY" = 1 ]; then
    echo "note: files were not verified (--no-verify)" >&2
  elif [ ! -s "$STAGE/sums" ]; then
    echo "ERROR: the text file has no @@CHECKSUMS section: it is incomplete (cut off while copying?) or written by hand. Nothing was written." >&2
    echo "       (use --no-verify to accept it anyway)" >&2
    return 1
  else
    echo "ERROR: sha256sum/shasum not found, so the files cannot be verified. Nothing was written. (use --no-verify to skip)" >&2
    return 1
  fi

  local total; total="$(sort -u "$STAGE/list" | grep -c . || true)"
  if [ "$LIST" = 1 ]; then
    sort -u "$STAGE/list"
    echo
    echo "$total file(s), $(grep -c . "$STAGE/removed" || true) to remove"
    return 0
  fi
  if [ "$total" = 0 ]; then echo "The hand-over holds no files."; return 0; fi

  # ---- 4. copy into the target ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  [ "$DRY" = 1 ] || mkdir -p "$TARGET"
  TARGET="$(cd "$TARGET" 2>/dev/null && pwd || printf '%s' "$TARGET")"
  local created=0 overwritten=0 unchanged=0 skipped=0 deferred=0 action src dst
  : > "$STAGE/applied"
  while IFS= read -r path; do
    src="$STAGE/files$path"; dst="$TARGET$path"
    if [ ! -e "$dst" ]; then action=created
    elif [ -f "$dst" ] && cmp -s "$src" "$dst"; then action=unchanged
    elif [ "$FORCE" = 1 ]; then action=overwritten
    else action=skipped; fi

    case "$action" in
      created|overwritten)
        if [ "$DRY" = 0 ]; then
          mkdir -p "$(dirname "$dst")"
          if [ "$dst" = "$SELF" ]; then
            # Do not replace the running script (Windows locks it): leave the new version next to it.
            cp "$src" "$dst.new"; deferred=1
            echo "  new version saved as ${path#/}.new  (run: mv ${path#/}.new ${path#/})"
          else
            cp "$src" "$dst"
          fi
          case "$path" in *.sh) chmod +x "$dst" 2>/dev/null || true ;; esac
        fi
        [ "$action" = created ] && created=$((created + 1)) || overwritten=$((overwritten + 1))
        echo "$path" >> "$STAGE/applied"
        ;;
      unchanged) unchanged=$((unchanged + 1)) ;;
      skipped) skipped=$((skipped + 1)); echo "  skipped (exists and differs): ${path#/}" ;;
    esac
  done < <(sort -u "$STAGE/list")

  printf '%s%d written (%d new, %d overwritten), %d already identical, %d skipped.\n' \
    "$([ "$DRY" = 1 ] && echo 'Dry run: ' || true)" "$((created + overwritten))" "$created" "$overwritten" "$unchanged" "$skipped"
  [ "$skipped" -gt 0 ] && echo "(use -f to overwrite existing files)"

  # ---- 5. files that no longer belong to the app -------------------------------------------------------------
  if [ -s "$STAGE/removed" ]; then
    local r any=0
    while IFS= read -r r; do
      if [ -e "$TARGET$r" ]; then
        if [ "$any" = 0 ]; then echo; echo "These files are no longer part of the app:"; any=1; fi
        if [ "$PRUNE" = 1 ] && [ "$DRY" = 0 ]; then rm -f "$TARGET$r" && echo "  deleted   ${r#/}"; else echo "  delete    ${r#/}"; fi
      fi
    done < "$STAGE/removed"
    if [ "$any" = 1 ] && [ "$PRUNE" != 1 ]; then echo "(run again with --prune to delete them, or delete them yourself)"; fi
  fi

  # ---- 6. what to do next, only when needed ------------------------------------------------------------------
  local NEXT=()
  grep -Eq '^/package(-lock)?\.json$' "$STAGE/applied" && NEXT+=("npm install              (use the configured internal npm repository)")
  grep -Eq '^/(src/|tsconfig|vitest\.config|eslint\.config)' "$STAGE/applied" && NEXT+=("npm run build            (source changed)")
  grep -Eq '^/tests/' "$STAGE/applied" && NEXT+=("npm test                 (tests changed)")
  grep -Eq '^/\.env\.example$' "$STAGE/applied" && NEXT+=("compare .env.example with your environment variables (new settings may have been added)")
  grep -Eq '^/(src/|package\.json|scripts/)' "$STAGE/applied" && NEXT+=("restart the app          (npm start)")
  if [ "${#NEXT[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
    echo
    echo "Next:"
    local n
    for n in "${NEXT[@]}"; do echo "  $n"; done
  fi
  echo "Target folder: $TARGET"
}

main "$@"; exit $?

/src/app.ts
import express, { type Express } from 'express';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import type { Container } from './container.js';
import baseRouter from './routers/index.js';
import { createErrorHandler, notFoundHandler } from './http/errors.js';

export const API_PREFIX = '/api/v1';

export function createApp(c: Container): Express {
  const app = express();
  app.disable('x-powered-by');
  app.use(express.json({ limit: '256kb' }));

  app.use((req, res, next) => {
    const incoming = req.header('x-request-id');
    const requestId = incoming && /^[A-Za-z0-9-]{8,64}$/.test(incoming) ? incoming : randomUUID();
    res.locals.requestId = requestId;
    res.setHeader('x-request-id', requestId);
    const started = Date.now();
    res.on('finish', () => {
      // Match request severity to the HTTP status.
      const outcome = res.statusCode >= 500 ? 'error' : res.statusCode >= 400 ? 'warn' : 'success';
      c.logger[outcome]('request', {
        method: req.method,
        // Preserve the full path when logging requests handled by nested routers.
        path: req.originalUrl.split('?')[0],
        status: res.statusCode,
        ms: Date.now() - started,
        requestId,
        soeid: res.locals.soeid as string | undefined,
      });
    });
    next();
  });

  const api = baseRouter(c);
  app.use(API_PREFIX, api);

  app.use(notFoundHandler);
  app.use(createErrorHandler(c.logger));
  return app;
}

/src/config/daily-logs.ts
import { appendFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { createReadStream } from 'node:fs';
import { createInterface } from 'node:readline';
import { resolve, join } from 'node:path';

export const logDirectory = (): string => resolve(process.env.LOG_DIRECTORY || 'logs');
let pending: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
export const flushDailyLogs = (): Promise<void> => pending;

/** Files always contain redacted JSON, independently of the console format. UTC date boundaries. */
export function appendDailyLog(line: string, time: Date): void {
  const directory = logDirectory();
  pending = pending
    .then(async () => {
      await mkdir(directory, { recursive: true });
      await appendFile(join(directory, `app-${time.toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.log`), `${line}\n`, {
        mode: 0o600,
      });
    })
    .catch(() => {
      process.stderr.write('Unable to write daily application log\n');
    });
}

export async function queryLogs(
  options: {
    days: number;
    limit: number;
    level?: string;
    search?: string;
    requestId?: string;
    soeid?: string;
  },
  now = new Date(),
) {
  await pending;
  const from = new Date(now.getTime() - options.days * 86400000);
  const entries: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];
  let scanned = 0;
  let truncated = false;
  // Newest files first, and retain the newest matching entries within each file.
  for (
    let day = new Date(now);
    day.toISOString().slice(0, 10) >= from.toISOString().slice(0, 10);
    day = new Date(day.getTime() - 86400000)
  ) {
    const input = createReadStream(join(logDirectory(), `app-${day.toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.log`), {
      encoding: 'utf8',
    });
    const lines = createInterface({ input, crlfDelay: Infinity });
    try {
      for await (const line of lines) {
        if (++scanned > 100000) {
          truncated = true;
          break;
        }
        let entry: Record<string, unknown>;
        try {
          entry = JSON.parse(line) as Record<string, unknown>;
        } catch {
          continue;
        }
        const time = Date.parse(String(entry.time));
        if (!Number.isFinite(time) || time < from.getTime() || time > now.getTime()) continue;
        if (options.level && entry.level !== options.level) continue;
        if (options.requestId && entry.requestId !== options.requestId) continue;
        if (options.soeid && entry.soeid !== options.soeid) continue;
        if (options.search && !line.toLowerCase().includes(options.search.toLowerCase())) continue;
        entries.push(entry);
      }
    } catch (error) {
      if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== 'ENOENT') throw error;
    } finally {
      lines.close();
      input.destroy();
    }
    if (truncated) break;
  }
  entries.sort((a, b) => String(b.time).localeCompare(String(a.time)));
  return {
    from: from.toISOString(),
    to: now.toISOString(),
    truncated: truncated || entries.length > options.limit,
    entries: entries.slice(0, options.limit),
  };
}

/src/config/env.ts
import { z } from 'zod';

const flag = (def: 'true' | 'false' = 'false') =>
  z
    .enum(['true', 'false'])
    .default(def)
    .transform((v) => v === 'true');

const intFrom = (def: number) => z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(def);

const schema = z.object({
  PORT: intFrom(3050),
  NODE_ENV: z.string().default('development'),
  LOG_LEVEL: z.enum(['debug', 'info', 'warn', 'error', 'silent']).default('info'),
  // pretty = readable lines for a terminal; json = one JSON object per line for log collectors
  LOG_FORMAT: z.enum(['pretty', 'json']).default('pretty'),
  // colours in the pretty format (errors red, warnings orange, successes green, the rest white); NO_COLOR turns them off
  LOG_COLOR: flag('true'),

  SECRET_KEY: z.string().min(16, 'SECRET_KEY must be at least 16 characters'),
  ADMIN_USERNAME: z.string().min(1, 'ADMIN_USERNAME is required'),
  ADMIN_PASSWORD: z.string().min(1, 'ADMIN_PASSWORD is required'),
  JWT_EXPIRES_IN: z.string().default('60d'),

  CLUSTER_API_URL: z.string().default(''),
  // Optional second cluster for the build/deploy projects; when unset the same cluster is used.
  DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL: z.string().optional(),
  // Explicit bearer tokens. When unset they are found by name: devSpacesClusterToken<id> / devOpsToken<id>.
  CLUSTER_TOKEN: z.string().optional(),
  DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN: z.string().optional(),
  KUBECONFIG_PATH: z.string().optional(),
  DEPLOY_PROJECT_REQUESTER: z.string().default('devspaces-support-team'),
  // When unset: https://devspaces.apps.<rest of the CLUSTER_API_URL host>
  DEV_SPACES_SERVICE_FQDN: z.string().optional(),

  DEVREG_URL: z.string().min(1, 'DEVREG_URL is required'),
  // Editor ids as the Dev Spaces dashboard knows them, e.g. che-incubator/che-code/latest (a bare name such as
  // `che-code` is accepted and expanded).
  EDITOR_VSCODE: z.string().default('che-incubator/che-code/latest'),
  EDITOR_INTELLIJ: z.string().default('che-incubator/che-idea/latest'),
  EDITOR_GOLAND: z.string().default('che-incubator/che-goland/latest'),
  WORKSPACE_PROXY_URL: z.string().optional(),

  MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES: intFrom(3),
  MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES: intFrom(5),

  POSTGRES_ENABLED: flag('true'),
  POSTGRES_HOSTNAME: z.string().default('localhost'),
  POSTGRES_PORT: intFrom(5432),
  POSTGRES_DATABASE: z.string().default(''),
  POSTGRES_USER: z.string().default(''),
  POSTGRES_PASSWORD: z.string().default(''),
  // The existing database requires TLS with a CA file chosen by NODE_ENV (see infra/db/pg-ssl.ts).
  POSTGRES_SSL: flag('true'),
  POSTGRES_CA_FILE: z.string().optional(),
  POSTGRES_CERT_DIR: z.string().optional(),
  POSTGRES_DATABASE_TABLENAME: z.string().default('metrics."devspaces-metrics"'),

  NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL: z.string().optional(),
  NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME: z.string().default(''),
  NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD: z.string().default(''),
  NOTIFICATION_SENDER: z.string().default('noreply@example.com'),
  NOTIFICATION_CC: z.string().optional(),
  EMAIL_DOMAIN: z.string().default('example.com'),
  USER_DIRECTORY_GRAPHQL_URL: z.string().optional(),

  GITHUB_URL: z.string().default('https://github.com'),
  GITHUB_USER_SUFFIX: z.string().default(''),
});

/** `'1524'` → `1524`, `"a b"` → `a b`; anything not wrapped in one matching pair of quotes is untouched. */
export function unquote(value: string): string {
  const match = /^(['"])(.*)\1$/s.exec(value);
  return match?.[2] ?? value;
}

/** `che-code` → `che-incubator/che-code/latest`; `che-incubator/che-code/latest` is kept as it is. */
export function normalizeEditorId(value: string): string {
  const v = value.trim();
  return v.includes('/') ? v : `che-incubator/${v}/latest`;
}

/** `https://api.namctigtd001d.ecs.dyn.example.net:6443` → `namctigtd001d`. */
export function clusterNameOf(apiUrl: string): string | undefined {
  try {
    const host = new URL(apiUrl).hostname;
    return host.startsWith('api.') ? host.split('.')[1] : undefined;
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}

/** `https://api.<rest>:6443` → `https://devspaces.apps.<rest>` (the usual Dev Spaces dashboard host). */
export function deriveDevSpacesFqdn(apiUrl: string): string {
  try {
    const host = new URL(apiUrl).hostname;
    return host.startsWith('api.') ? `https://devspaces.apps.${host.slice('api.'.length)}` : '';
  } catch {
    return '';
  }
}

export interface FoundToken {
  /** The environment variable the token came from (never the token itself). */
  name: string;
  value: string;
}

/**
 * Finds the bearer token for a cluster by variable name: `<prefix><id>`, e.g. devSpacesClusterToken001d for the cluster
 * `namctigtd001d` (the id must be the end of the cluster name). A variable without an id (`<prefix>`) fits any cluster.
 * Tokens that belong to another cluster are ignored, so a leftover `devOpsToken004d` is never sent to cluster 001d.
 */
export function findClusterToken(
  env: Record<string, string | undefined>,
  prefix: string,
  apiUrl: string,
): FoundToken | undefined {
  const cluster = (clusterNameOf(apiUrl) ?? '').toLowerCase();
  const fits = Object.keys(env)
    .filter((k) => k.toLowerCase().startsWith(prefix.toLowerCase()) && env[k])
    .filter((k) => {
      const id = k.slice(prefix.length).toLowerCase();
      return id === '' || (cluster !== '' && cluster.endsWith(id));
    });
  if (fits.length > 1) {
    throw new Error(
      `Invalid configuration:\n  - several variables fit one cluster token: ${fits.join(', ')}`,
    );
  }
  const name = fits[0];
  return name ? { name, value: env[name] as string } : undefined;
}

export interface AppConfig {
  port: number;
  nodeEnv: string;
  logLevel: 'debug' | 'info' | 'warn' | 'error' | 'silent';
  logFormat: 'pretty' | 'json';
  /** ANSI colours in the pretty log format. */
  logColor: boolean;
  auth: { secretKey: string; adminUsername: string; adminPassword: string; expiresIn: string };
  cluster: {
    /** The Dev Spaces cluster. */
    apiUrl: string;
    /** Bearer token for it; when unset the kubeconfig / in-cluster service account is used. */
    token?: string;
    /** Name of the variable the token came from, for the startup log (never the token). */
    tokenVar?: string;
    /** Cluster for build/deploy projects: DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL, or the same cluster as apiUrl. */
    deployApiUrl: string;
    /** Only set when the deploy cluster is a different one. */
    deployToken?: string;
    deployTokenVar?: string;
    /** False when NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0: certificates of the clusters are then not verified. */
    tlsRejectUnauthorized: boolean;
    kubeconfigPath?: string;
    deployProjectRequester: string;
    devSpacesServiceFqdn: string;
  };
  devreg: {
    url: string;
    editors: { vscode: string; intellij: string; goland: string };
    workspaceProxyUrl?: string;
  };
  limits: { maxRunning: number; maxTotal: number };
  postgres: {
    enabled: boolean;
    host: string;
    port: number;
    database: string;
    user: string;
    password: string;
    ssl: boolean;
    /** Explicit CA bundle; when unset the file is chosen from NODE_ENV (dev.pem / uat.pem / prod.pem). */
    caFile?: string;
    certDir?: string;
    /** NODE_ENV, which selects the CA file and whether the hostname check is relaxed (development only). */
    env: string;
    /** Existing metrics table, e.g. metrics."devspaces-metrics-dev". The service only ever inserts into it. */
    table: string;
  };
  notification: {
    url?: string;
    username: string;
    password: string;
    sender: string;
    cc?: string;
    emailDomain: string;
    userDirectoryUrl?: string;
  };
  github: { url: string; userSuffix: string };
}

/** What the service understood from its environment, for the startup log. Contains no secret values. */
export function startupSummary(c: AppConfig): Record<string, unknown> {
  const auth = (tokenVar: string | undefined, kubeconfig: string | undefined): string =>
    tokenVar
      ? `bearer token from ${tokenVar}`
      : kubeconfig
        ? 'kubeconfig file'
        : 'default kubeconfig / pod service account';
  return {
    port: c.port,
    nodeEnv: c.nodeEnv,
    cluster: c.cluster.apiUrl,
    clusterAuth: auth(c.cluster.tokenVar, c.cluster.kubeconfigPath),
    deployCluster: c.cluster.deployApiUrl,
    deployClusterAuth:
      c.cluster.deployApiUrl === c.cluster.apiUrl
        ? 'same cluster'
        : auth(c.cluster.deployTokenVar, c.cluster.kubeconfigPath),
    devSpacesFqdn: c.cluster.devSpacesServiceFqdn,
    devfileRegistry: c.devreg.url,
    editors: c.devreg.editors,
    clusterTls: c.cluster.tlsRejectUnauthorized
      ? 'certificates verified'
      : 'NOT verified (NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0)',
    postgres: c.postgres.enabled
      ? {
          host: c.postgres.host,
          port: c.postgres.port,
          database: c.postgres.database,
          table: c.postgres.table,
        }
      : 'disabled',
  };
}

/** Parse and validate the environment once, at startup. Throws a readable error listing every problem. */
export function loadConfig(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): AppConfig {
  // An exported-but-empty variable (`FOO=`) is treated as unset. Values that still carry their shell quotes
  // (`'1524'`, `'metrics."devspaces-metrics-dev"'` from a .env file or a pasted export line) are unwrapped.
  const cleaned = Object.fromEntries(
    Object.entries(env)
      .filter(([, v]) => v !== undefined && v !== '')
      .map(([k, v]) => [k, unquote(v as string)]),
  );
  const parsed = schema.safeParse(cleaned);
  if (!parsed.success) {
    const problems = parsed.error.issues.map((i) => `${i.path.join('.') || '(root)'}: ${i.message}`);
    throw new Error(`Invalid configuration:\n  - ${problems.join('\n  - ')}`);
  }
  const e = parsed.data;
  if (e.POSTGRES_ENABLED && (!e.POSTGRES_DATABASE || !e.POSTGRES_USER)) {
    throw new Error(
      'Invalid configuration:\n  - POSTGRES_DATABASE and POSTGRES_USER are required when POSTGRES_ENABLED=true',
    );
  }

  const deployApiUrl = e.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL ?? e.CLUSTER_API_URL;
  const separateDeployCluster = deployApiUrl !== e.CLUSTER_API_URL;
  const main = e.CLUSTER_TOKEN
    ? { name: 'CLUSTER_TOKEN', value: e.CLUSTER_TOKEN }
    : findClusterToken(cleaned, 'devSpacesClusterToken', e.CLUSTER_API_URL);
  const deploy = !separateDeployCluster
    ? undefined
    : e.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN
      ? { name: 'DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN', value: e.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN }
      : findClusterToken(cleaned, 'devOpsToken', deployApiUrl);
  if (separateDeployCluster && !deploy && !e.KUBECONFIG_PATH) {
    throw new Error(
      `Invalid configuration:\n  - DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL is set to a different cluster but no token was found for it ` +
        `(set DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN, or devOpsToken<id> where <id> ends the cluster name)`,
    );
  }

  return {
    port: e.PORT,
    nodeEnv: e.NODE_ENV,
    logLevel: e.LOG_LEVEL,
    logFormat: e.LOG_FORMAT,
    logColor: e.LOG_COLOR && !cleaned.NO_COLOR,
    auth: {
      secretKey: e.SECRET_KEY,
      adminUsername: e.ADMIN_USERNAME,
      adminPassword: e.ADMIN_PASSWORD,
      expiresIn: e.JWT_EXPIRES_IN,
    },
    cluster: {
      apiUrl: e.CLUSTER_API_URL,
      token: main?.value,
      tokenVar: main?.name,
      deployApiUrl,
      deployToken: deploy?.value,
      deployTokenVar: deploy?.name,
      tlsRejectUnauthorized: cleaned.NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED !== '0',
      kubeconfigPath: e.KUBECONFIG_PATH,
      deployProjectRequester: e.DEPLOY_PROJECT_REQUESTER,
      devSpacesServiceFqdn: e.DEV_SPACES_SERVICE_FQDN ?? deriveDevSpacesFqdn(e.CLUSTER_API_URL),
    },
    devreg: {
      url: e.DEVREG_URL,
      editors: {
        vscode: normalizeEditorId(e.EDITOR_VSCODE),
        intellij: normalizeEditorId(e.EDITOR_INTELLIJ),
        goland: normalizeEditorId(e.EDITOR_GOLAND),
      },
      workspaceProxyUrl: e.WORKSPACE_PROXY_URL,
    },
    limits: { maxRunning: e.MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES, maxTotal: e.MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES },
    postgres: {
      enabled: e.POSTGRES_ENABLED,
      host: e.POSTGRES_HOSTNAME,
      port: e.POSTGRES_PORT,
      database: e.POSTGRES_DATABASE,
      user: e.POSTGRES_USER,
      password: e.POSTGRES_PASSWORD,
      ssl: e.POSTGRES_SSL,
      caFile: e.POSTGRES_CA_FILE,
      certDir: e.POSTGRES_CERT_DIR,
      env: e.NODE_ENV,
      table: e.POSTGRES_DATABASE_TABLENAME,
    },
    notification: {
      url: e.NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL,
      username: e.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME,
      password: e.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD,
      sender: e.NOTIFICATION_SENDER,
      cc: e.NOTIFICATION_CC,
      emailDomain: e.EMAIL_DOMAIN,
      userDirectoryUrl: e.USER_DIRECTORY_GRAPHQL_URL,
    },
    github: { url: e.GITHUB_URL.replace(/\/$/, ''), userSuffix: e.GITHUB_USER_SUFFIX },
  };
}

/src/config/runtime-settings.ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { deriveDevSpacesFqdn, type AppConfig } from './env.js';
import { BadRequestError } from '../utils/errors.js';

const url = z.url({ protocol: /^https?$/ }).max(2000);
export const runtimeSettingsSchema = z
  .strictObject({
    DEVREG_URL: url.optional(),
    NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL: url.optional(),
    NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME: z.string().max(200).optional(),
    NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD: z.string().max(4000).optional(),
    MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES: z.number().int().positive().optional(),
    MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES: z.number().int().positive().optional(),
    CLUSTER_API_URL: url.optional(),
    CLUSTER_TOKEN: z.string().min(1).max(20000).optional(),
    DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL: url.optional(),
    DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN: z.string().min(1).max(20000).optional(),
  })
  .refine((value) => Object.keys(value).length > 0, 'At least one setting is required');

export const runtimeSettingsView = (current: AppConfig) => ({
  DEVREG_URL: current.devreg.url,
  NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL: current.notification.url,
  NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME: current.notification.username,
  NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD: current.notification.password ? '[redacted]' : '',
  MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES: current.limits.maxRunning,
  MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES: current.limits.maxTotal,
  CLUSTER_API_URL: current.cluster.apiUrl,
  CLUSTER_TOKEN: current.cluster.token ? '[redacted]' : '',
  DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL: current.cluster.deployApiUrl,
  DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN: current.cluster.deployToken ? '[redacted]' : '',
});

export function applyRuntimeSettings(
  current: AppConfig,
  body: z.infer<typeof runtimeSettingsSchema>,
): AppConfig {
  if (body.CLUSTER_API_URL && body.CLUSTER_API_URL !== current.cluster.apiUrl && !body.CLUSTER_TOKEN)
    throw new BadRequestError('Changing the cluster URL requires its token in the same request');
  if (
    body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL &&
    body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL !== current.cluster.deployApiUrl &&
    !body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN
  )
    throw new BadRequestError('Changing the deployment cluster URL requires its token in the same request');
  const config = structuredClone(current);
  if (body.DEVREG_URL !== undefined) config.devreg.url = body.DEVREG_URL;
  if (body.NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL !== undefined) config.notification.url = body.NOTIFICATION_MAIL_URL;
  if (body.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME !== undefined)
    config.notification.username = body.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_USERNAME;
  if (body.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD !== undefined)
    config.notification.password = body.NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_PASSWORD;
  if (body.MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES !== undefined) config.limits.maxRunning = body.MAX_RUNNING_WORKSPACES;
  if (body.MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES !== undefined) config.limits.maxTotal = body.MAX_TOTAL_WORKSPACES;
  if (config.limits.maxRunning > config.limits.maxTotal)
    throw new BadRequestError('Maximum running workspaces cannot exceed maximum total workspaces');
  if (body.CLUSTER_API_URL !== undefined) {
    config.cluster.apiUrl = body.CLUSTER_API_URL;
    config.cluster.devSpacesServiceFqdn = deriveDevSpacesFqdn(body.CLUSTER_API_URL);
  }
  if (body.CLUSTER_TOKEN !== undefined) config.cluster.token = body.CLUSTER_TOKEN;
  if (body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL !== undefined) config.cluster.deployApiUrl = body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_API_URL;
  else if (current.cluster.deployApiUrl === current.cluster.apiUrl)
    config.cluster.deployApiUrl = config.cluster.apiUrl;
  if (body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN !== undefined) config.cluster.deployToken = body.DEPLOY_CLUSTER_TOKEN;
  if (
    config.cluster.deployApiUrl !== config.cluster.apiUrl &&
    !config.cluster.deployToken &&
    !config.cluster.kubeconfigPath
  )
    throw new BadRequestError('A separate deployment cluster requires a token or kubeconfig');
  return config;
}

/src/container.ts
import type { AppConfig } from './config/env.js';
import { MetricsRepository, NoopMetricsStore, createPgPool } from './infra/db/MetricsRepository.js';
import { DevfileRegistryClient, createRegistryHttp } from './infra/devreg/DevfileRegistryClient.js';
import { EmailNotifier, NoopNotifier } from './infra/email/EmailNotifier.js';
import { GraphqlUserDirectory, NullUserDirectory } from './infra/email/GraphqlUserDirectory.js';
import { KubeClient, createKubeConfig } from './infra/kube/KubeClient.js';
import {
  systemClock,
  type Clock,
  type DevfileRegistry,
  type KubeGateway,
  type MetricsStore,
  type Notifier,
} from './infra/ports.js';
import { DeploymentNamespaceService } from './services/DeploymentNamespaceService.js';
import { GitConfigService } from './services/GitConfigService.js';
import { MetricsRecorder } from './services/MetricsRecorder.js';
import { NamespaceService } from './services/NamespaceService.js';
import { PatService } from './services/PatService.js';
import { WorkspaceService } from './services/WorkspaceService.js';
import { SupportService } from './services/SupportService.js';
import type { Logger } from './utils/logger.js';

export interface Dependencies {
  kube: KubeGateway;
  /** Cluster for build/deploy projects when it is a different one; defaults to `kube`. */
  deployKube?: KubeGateway;
  metrics: MetricsStore;
  registry: DevfileRegistry;
  notifier: Notifier;
  clock?: Clock;
}

export interface Container {
  reconfigure(config: AppConfig): void;
  config: AppConfig;
  logger: Logger;
  kube: KubeGateway;
  /** Same object as `kube` unless a separate deploy cluster is configured. */
  deployKube: KubeGateway;
  metrics: MetricsStore;
  registry: DevfileRegistry;
  recorder: MetricsRecorder;
  namespaces: NamespaceService;
  workspaces: WorkspaceService;
  pats: PatService;
  gitConfig: GitConfigService;
  deployments: DeploymentNamespaceService;
  support: SupportService;
}

/** Wire services to production adapters or test doubles. */
export function buildContainer(
  config: AppConfig,
  logger: Logger,
  deps: Dependencies,
  refresh?: (config: AppConfig) => Dependencies,
): Container {
  const clock = deps.clock ?? systemClock;
  const recorder = new MetricsRecorder(deps.metrics, clock, logger);
  const namespaces = new NamespaceService(
    deps.kube,
    recorder,
    { workspaceProxyUrl: config.devreg.workspaceProxyUrl, emailDomain: config.notification.emailDomain },
    logger,
  );
  const deployments = new DeploymentNamespaceService(
    deps.deployKube ?? deps.kube,
    recorder,
    deps.notifier,
    config.cluster.deployProjectRequester,
    logger,
  );
  const container: Container = {
    reconfigure(next) {
      const nextDependencies = refresh ? refresh(next) : deps;
      const reconfigure = container.reconfigure;
      Object.assign(container, buildContainer(next, logger, nextDependencies, refresh), { reconfigure });
    },
    config,
    logger,
    kube: deps.kube,
    deployKube: deps.deployKube ?? deps.kube,
    metrics: deps.metrics,
    registry: deps.registry,
    recorder,
    namespaces,
    workspaces: new WorkspaceService(
      deps.kube,
      namespaces,
      deps.registry,
      recorder,
      {
        limits: config.limits,
        editors: config.devreg.editors,
        devSpacesServiceFqdn: config.cluster.devSpacesServiceFqdn,
      },
      clock,
      logger,
    ),
    pats: new PatService(deps.kube, namespaces, recorder, config.github),
    gitConfig: new GitConfigService(deps.kube, namespaces, recorder),
    deployments,
    support: new SupportService({
      kube: deps.kube,
      deployKube: deps.deployKube ?? deps.kube,
      namespaces,
      deployments,
      logger,
    }),
  };
  return container;
}

/** Real adapters for a running pod. */
export function createProductionDependencies(config: AppConfig, logger: Logger): Dependencies {
  const directory = config.notification.userDirectoryUrl
    ? new GraphqlUserDirectory(config.notification.userDirectoryUrl, logger)
    : new NullUserDirectory();
  const { cluster } = config;
  const skipTlsVerify = !cluster.tlsRejectUnauthorized;
  const separateDeploy = cluster.deployApiUrl !== cluster.apiUrl;
  return {
    kube: new KubeClient(
      createKubeConfig({
        apiUrl: cluster.apiUrl,
        token: cluster.token,
        kubeconfigPath: cluster.kubeconfigPath,
        skipTlsVerify,
      }),
    ),
    deployKube: separateDeploy
      ? new KubeClient(
          createKubeConfig({
            apiUrl: cluster.deployApiUrl,
            token: cluster.deployToken,
            kubeconfigPath: cluster.kubeconfigPath,
            skipTlsVerify,
          }),
        )
      : undefined,
    metrics: config.postgres.enabled
      ? new MetricsRepository(createPgPool(config.postgres, logger), config.postgres.table, logger)
      : new NoopMetricsStore(),
    registry: new DevfileRegistryClient(createRegistryHttp(config.devreg.url)),
    notifier: config.notification.url
      ? new EmailNotifier(config.notification, config.cluster.deployApiUrl, directory, logger)
      : new NoopNotifier(),
  };
}

/src/controllers/authController.ts
import type { RequestHandler } from 'express';
import type { Container } from '../container.js';
import { checkAdminCredentials, signToken } from '../http/auth.js';
import { loginSchema, parse } from '../http/schemas.js';
import { UnauthorizedError } from '../utils/errors.js';

export function authController(container: Container) {
  return {
    login: (req, res, next) => {
      try {
        const { username, password } = parse(loginSchema, req.body);
        if (!checkAdminCredentials(container.config.auth, username, password)) {
          throw new UnauthorizedError('Username or password is incorrect');
        }
        res.json({ token: signToken(container.config.auth, { soeid: username, role: 'admin' }) });
      } catch (error) {
        next(error);
      }
    },
  } satisfies Record<string, RequestHandler>;
}

/src/controllers/configurationController.ts
import type { Container } from '../container.js';
import {
  runtimeSettingsSchema,
  runtimeSettingsView,
  applyRuntimeSettings,
} from '../config/runtime-settings.js';

import { parse } from '../http/schemas.js';

export function configurationController(container: Container) {

    view: asyncHandler(async (_req, res) => {
      res.json({ settings: runtimeSettingsView(container.config), persistence: 'process-lifetime' });

/src/controllers/deploymentNamespaceController.ts
import type { Request, RequestHandler, Response } from 'express';
import { optionalCsiid } from '../http/helpers.js';
import type { Container } from '../container.js';
import { getSoeid } from '../http/auth.js';
import { asyncHandler } from '../http/errors.js';
import { csiidBodySchema, parse } from '../http/schemas.js';
import { csiidFromDeploymentNamespace, parseCsiid } from '../utils/names.js';

export function deploymentNamespaceController(container: Container) {
  return {
    list: asyncHandler(async (req, res) => {
      const csiid = optionalCsiid(req);
      const projects = await container.deployments.list(getSoeid(res));
      res.json(csiid ? projects.filter((name) => csiidFromDeploymentNamespace(name) === csiid) : projects);
    }),
    create: asyncHandler(async (req, res) => {
      const { csiid } = parse(csiidBodySchema, req.body);
      const result = await container.deployments.ensure(getSoeid(res), parseCsiid(csiid));
      res.status(result.exists ? 200 : 201).json(result);
    }),
    remove: asyncHandler(async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
      res.json(await container.deployments.delete(getSoeid(res)));
    }),
  } satisfies Record<string, RequestHandler>;
}

import type { RequestHandler } from 'express';
import type { Container } from '../container.js';

export function gitConfigController(container: Container) {

    view: asyncHandler(async (req, res) => {

    }),

    }),
  } satisfies Record<string, RequestHandler>;
